Paper I — J.A.R.V.I.S.: An Operating System for the HgX Estate

VERSION 1.2 · FULL TEXT

W H I T E PA P E R / V E R S I O N 1 . 2

J.A.R.V.I.S. An operating system for the HgX estate — open source from day one One principal. A federation of durable roles. Current facts, inspectable evidence, and human authority at every consequential boundary. Built in the open, because a system that demands to be trusted must be inspectable.

Purpose

Operating model

Date

Architecture and governance

Human-led, agent-executed, open source

October 2026

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

J.A.R.V.I.S. is an operating system for accepted work The objective is not to build a more talkative assistant. It is to create a dependable personal intelligence system that turns one instruction into completed, reviewable work while leaving authority with HgX. THE PROPOSITION

THE CONTROL MODEL

J.A.R.V.I.S. coordinates a set of durable specialist roles across the HgX estate. Each role owns one job, reads current facts from authoritative systems, uses the most reliable available tool, produces an artifact, returns evidence, and stops before a consequential action unless HgX has approved it.

Memory carries identity, standards, working preferences, and continuity. Live sources carry changing facts. Skills encode methods that have already worked. Routines schedule only work that has survived review. Evidence converts fluent claims into inspectable results.

This design replaces a monolithic “AI helper” with an estate of accountable operators. It allows context to compound without allowing ownership to blur.

The final control surface remains human: prepare first, show target and effect, then pause at the last safe moment.

The scarce resource is not compute. It is HgX's attention. A mature J.A.R.V.I.S. protects that attention by speaking when there is signal, remaining silent when there is none, and never substituting confidence for proof. DESIGN PRINCIPLES Truth over comfort

Discretion

Reversibility

Stewardship

Foundation — open source from day one. A fifth principle stands alongside the four above: doctrine, skills, contracts, and reference implementation are produced to be publishable. The full statement follows.

Executive summary

THE MAIN VISION / OPEN SOURCE

Open source is the main vision, not a release strategy Version 1.2 places one vision at the centre of the whole system: J.A.R.V.I.S. is built in the open, from the first day, so that anyone can inspect how it works, test whether it deserves trust, and build on it.

Open source from day one. J.A.R.V.I.S. is built in the open as a matter of foundation, not as a release strategy. Every aim of the programme — doctrine, skills, contracts, reference implementation — is produced to be publishable, because a system that demands to be trusted must be inspectable. The boundary is absolute: the system goes public; the principal's private life never does. These papers describe a running system, not a proposal. WHAT GOES PUBLIC The doctrine and the principles that govern it Skills as versioned, reusable operating contracts

WHAT NEVER GOES PUBLIC The principal's private life, family, and household Finances, credentials, and personal records

Role, source, evidence, approval, and continuity contracts

Live estate data, messages, and private memory

The reference implementation, its tests, and its history

Anything whose disclosure would weaken security

These white papers — vision, technical, and front-end

The sanitising pass is real work: only the system is publishable.

A RUNNING SYSTEM, NOT A PROPOSAL This paper does not describe a hypothetical assistant. It describes the live J.A.R.V.I.S. system in service of the HgX estate — the reference implementation is the estate itself, running under this doctrine every day, with hot-swappable intelligence engines beneath stable, published contracts. Openness also disciplines the builders: work that must be publishable is written more clearly, tested more honestly, and kept free of hidden assumptions.

Trust is not asserted. It is inspected.

Vision / Open source from day one

01 / VISION

Leverage should increase without surrendering authority J.A.R.V.I.S. exists to extend the principal's reach across the estate, not to replace the principal's judgment.

0 1 / A N T I C I PAT E

NOTICE WHAT IS BECOMING RELEVANT Maintain situational awareness across commitments, health, communications, markets, sports, documents, and operations. Escalate genuine signal; suppress routine noise.

0 2 / P R E PA R E

DO THE REVERSIBLE WORK FIRST Collect facts, draft outputs, reconcile records, test assumptions, and assemble decision packets before requesting attention.

03 / EXECUTE

04 / LEARN

Once approval is explicit, act through the narrowest reliable path, verify the result, and preserve the evidence needed to audit it later.

Convert proven methods into reusable skills, then schedule only those skills that remain reliable on fresh, live inputs.

COMPLETE APPROVED ACTIONS CLEANLY

TURN SUCCESSFUL WORK INTO CAPABILITY

The standard is calm competence: fewer supervisory exchanges, fewer repeated explanations, fewer hidden assumptions, and more work that passes review on first inspection. SYSTEM BOUNDARY J.A.R.V.I.S. is larger than any one model, application, or device. Muse is one operating component alongside INSTINCT ai and a Grok bot. The architecture therefore treats intelligence engines as replaceable participants beneath a stable estate doctrine: the role, source, evidence, approval, and continuity contracts survive changes in the underlying model. Because those contracts are the system, they are the part that is published. Open source is how leverage compounds beyond one estate: other operators can adopt the doctrine, inspect the reference implementation, and improve it, without ever gaining access to this estate. Design implication: the system's identity lives in its principles, records, role contracts, and acceptance standards - not in a single model session.

Vision / Leverage with authority

02 / SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE

The estate is a federation of narrow roles, not one monolith A durable role compounds context around a single owned job. The orchestration layer protects boundaries, resolves handoffs, and preserves one visible owner for every decision. PRINCIPAL

HgX sets intent, constraints, priorities, and final authority. Consequences remain reviewable.

ORCHESTRATOR

J.A.R.V.I.S. interprets the brief, chooses the role, enforces doctrine, coordinates evidence, and returns decisions rather than chatter.

ROLE ESTATE

Health monitoring

Sports and betting desk

Tax documents and returns

Macro estate administration

The Watchtower Live work-status watch

CAPABILITY

Skills, connectors, browser workflows, files, and computation. The route is selected by reliability, sensitivity, and fit for the task.

INTELLIGENCE

Muse, INSTINCT ai, Grok bot, and future engines. Engines can specialize or change without rewriting the estate's governing doctrine.

Boundary rule: role separation is an accountability mechanism, not proof of technical isolation. Shared files, sessions, connectors, or credentials require independent security controls. Open architecture: the federation is described in published contracts, not in private folklore. A new role, engine, or operator should be able to understand the architecture from the public papers and repository alone.

Architecture / A federation of roles

0 3 / O P E R AT I N G D O C T R I N E

Seven stages turn intent into accepted work The operating doctrine is a chain of custody for work. Each stage answers a different question; skipping one creates a predictable failure.

Keep roles narrow. Keep facts current. Keep proof visible. Keep consequences reviewable. Keep the method publishable. Every stage of the chain produces something an outside reviewer could, in principle, inspect. A method that cannot survive publication is not finished doctrine; it is a private habit.

Doctrine / Chain of custody for work

04 / DEPLOYMENT

DEPLOYMENT — The Autonomous Project Team When a project needs more than one pair of hands, J.A.R.V.I.S. deploys an autonomous specialist team under the Chief-of-Staff pattern. One principal, one orchestrator, one accountable return. HgX briefs J.A.R.V.I.S.; the orchestrator assembles the minimum team; the team returns only when the project is done, blocked, or needs HgX's approval.

THE BRIEF ESTABLISHES THE FINISH LINE PROJECT

THE TEAM IS BUILT AROUND REAL JOBS Break the objective into clear, measurable jobs.

Prefer existing estate bots and proven skills.

Identify which jobs can run in parallel.

Do not add agents because they merely sound useful.

Create only the specialists needed now. Assign one agent to one clear job.

Never duplicate roles or ownership.

Keep the team as small and focused as possible.

STANDING RULES No proof, no done.

Deployment / Verified completion and handoff

05 / MEMORY AND CONTINUITY

Memory preserves identity; live systems preserve truth Continuity is essential, but remembered state must never impersonate current state. KEEP IN DURABLE MEMORY

Preserve only durable outcomes, decisions, and next obligations.

CONTINUITY CONTRACT Every handoff should move the original brief, changed files or records, evidence, unresolved gaps, and the next safe action together. A new engine or specialist should be able to resume without reconstructing the entire conversation and without inheriting a hidden assumption as fact.

Memory answers “how do we work?” Live systems answer “what is true now?” The distinction is a control, not a convenience. Retention discipline: preserve enough to maintain service and accountability; do not collect sensitive context merely because storage is available. Publication boundary: memory is where the boundary is enforced in practice. Durable methods, role definitions, and principles belong to the system and are publishable. Personal memory — preferences tied to a private life, household detail, and lived history — stays in the estate and is never part of a public release.

Memory / Identity without stale state

0 6 / A P P R OVA L A N D S E C U R I T Y

Approval belongs at the last safe moment The system should complete every reversible preparation step, then pause immediately before an action becomes external, binding, destructive, or difficult to undo.

THE APPROVAL GATE Prepare: draft the message, assemble the purchase, stage the change, or identify the exact record.

STOP

Present: show the target, effect, cost, scope, and evidence. Pause: require explicit approval for the specific consequence. Execute and verify: act once, read the result, and report what actually happened.

ACTION CLASS

DEFAULT POSTURE

APPROVAL EVIDENCE

Read / analyse

Proceed within task scope

Source and access path

Draft / prepare

Proceed reversibly

Artifact plus intended target

Send / publish

Stop before transmission

Exact content and destination

Purchase / transfer

Stop before commitment

Item, amount, recipient, effect

Delete / revoke

Prefer recoverable change

Object, scope, recovery path

Permissions / terms

Stop before acceptance

Access change and beneficiary

SECURITY POSTURE Least privilege, need-to-know disclosure, authenticated destinations, and auditable outcomes are system requirements. Credentials remain in approved secure channels. Shared infrastructure is never assumed to provide isolation merely because roles have different names. Standing estate rule: no message is sent, no purchase is made, and no data is deleted without HgX's explicit approval for that exact action. Open does not mean exposed: the system is published; credentials, live data, and principal are not.

Control / The human stop sign

07 / EVIDENCE AND ROUTINES

Evidence is the unit of trust A fluent status update cannot establish completion. A reviewable evidence packet can. ARTIFACT

The file, draft, ledger, report, or changed record.

SOURCES

Where every material fact came from.

CHANGES

What the run actually modified.

TESTS

What was checked and what passed.

APPROVALS

What remains waiting on HgX.

GAPS

What is uncertain, stale, blocked, or incomplete.

ACC E P TA N C E T E S T

DONE IS OBSERVABLE A role may call work complete only when the artifact exists, its required checks have passed, and the evidence permits an independent reviewer to verify the result without reconstructing the chat. Failures are first-class outputs. A missing source, blocked action, uncertain identity, or failed check must remain visible rather than being paved over with plausible language.

AUTOMATION EARNS ITS PLACE

ONE-TIME TASK Prove the outcome manually.

STABLE SKILL

›

Encode inputs, rules, failures, and return format.

ROUTINE

TEST RUN

›

Validate on safe, live data.

›

Schedule with source, cadence, and stop rule.

Scheduled services remain silent by default. Quiet days should stay quiet. A routine interrupts HgX only when a stated threshold is met, an approval is required, or the source has failed in a way that threatens the outcome. Evidence is also the bridge to open source: the same packet that lets HgX verify a run lets an external reviewer verify the system. Inspectability inside the estate and inspectability in public are one discipline, applied twice.

Evidence / Proof before repetition

0 8 / O P E R AT O R S C O R E C A R D

SCALE WHEN

The role repeatedly clears review.

A routine produces noise without decisions.

Human repair is low and declining.

Confidence rises while evidence weakens.

Evidence is complete and easy to inspect.

A role expands beyond its owned job.

Approval behaviour is predictable.

Human repair becomes the hidden workflow.

Fresh live data does not break the method.

Shared access is mistaken for permission.

Duplicate a proven operating lane. Do not scale merely by adding another bot.

Scorecard / Outcomes over motion

09 / ROADMAP

Scale the doctrine before expanding autonomy The next stage is not more personalities. It is stronger contracts between roles, sources, evidence, and authority. 1

2

3

4

5

6

7

8

CODIFY THE ESTATE REGISTRY Give every role a versioned contract: owned job, finish line, live sources, tools, output, cadence, acceptance test, and approval boundary.

STANDARDIZE EVIDENCE PACKETS Adopt a common result envelope for artifacts, provenance, changes, tests, gaps, and next decisions. Make handoffs machine-readable without making them unreadable to HgX.

SEPARATE CONTINUITY FROM CURRENT STATE Map each fact class to its authoritative system, set freshness expectations, and reconcile changes before action. Preserve durable memory selectively.

BUILD AN APPROVAL BROKER Present target, effect, cost, reversibility, and evidence in one decision surface. Bind approval to the exact action, not to a general intention.

GOVERN ROUTINES BY THE SCORECARD Retire noisy services, re-test changed dependencies, and automate only the preparation that repeatedly clears review.

FEDERATE INTELLIGENCE ENGINES Connect Muse, INSTINCT ai, Grok bot, and future engines through stable role and evidence contracts. Route work by capability while keeping one accountable owner.

ADVANCE AUTONOMY BY EARNED TRUST Expand scope only where accepted work, low repair, clean evidence, and predictable approvals remain strong over repeated live runs.

PUBLISH THE REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATION IN THE OPEN Complete the sanitising pass, publish doctrine, skills, contracts, and the reference implementation under an open licence, and maintain them in public. Publication timing remains on HgX's word; publishability is the standing requirement from day one.

Roadmap / Doctrine before autonomy

10 / CLOSING POSITION

The destination is calm competence J.A.R.V.I.S. succeeds when the estate becomes easier to govern: important matters arrive prepared, routine work completes quietly, evidence is always available, and authority never becomes ambiguous. WHAT REMAINS CONSTANT

WHAT MAY CHANGE

HgX defines purpose. Roles remain narrow. Current facts come from live systems. Evidence precedes trust. Consequences remain reviewable. The system protects attention rather than competing for it. And the system is open: built in the open from day one, inspectable by anyone who wishes to verify it.

Models, tools, interfaces, devices, and vendors will improve or disappear. They are components. The estate doctrine is the durable asset.

The ultimate J.A.R.V.I.S. is not the system that acts most often. It is the system that knows what to prepare, what to prove, when to ask, and when to remain silent.

FOUNDATION This architecture draws on the operating doctrine in Grok Bot: The One-Person Company, an independent twelve-step field guide supplied by HgX, and on the estate's existing operating practice. Reference document: Google Drive source. Open-source foundation (v1.2): Open source from day one. J.A.R.V.I.S. is built in the open as a matter of foundation, not as a release strategy. Every aim of the programme — doctrine, skills, contracts, reference implementation — is produced to be publishable, because a system that demands to be trusted must be inspectable. The boundary is absolute: the system goes public; the principal's private life never does. These papers describe a running system, not a proposal. Scope note: This paper defines an operating architecture and governance model for a running system. It is not a claim of technical isolation, a security certification, or a representation that every roadmap component is already integrated. Public release of the repository follows the sanitising pass and occurs only on HgX's word.

Contents and scope

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

SYSTEM THESIS

Reliability comes from contracts around the model J.A.R.V.I.S. is a single-orchestrator agent operating system on a persistent Linux virtual machine. The language model plans and judges; durable files, narrowly scoped skills, schedules, approvals, and evidence make the work reviewable. It is open source from day one: every contract in this paper is written to be inspected, reproduced, and improved in public. CONTROL PLANE

The engineering invariants INVARIANT

OPERATIONAL MEANING

No proof, no done

Delegation output is reviewed against artifacts, sources, tests, approvals, and gaps before completion is reported.

Current facts stay live

Memory carries durable context; changing state is re-read from its owning system.

Automate preparation first

One-time task becomes a proven skill, then a tested routine. Consequential execution remains approval-gated.

Persistence is explicit

Home-directory state survives restarts. System paths and /tmp are treated as disposable.

Failure is visible

Stale sources, exhausted retries, unverified handoffs, and missing permissions surface as failures or escalation.

Built to be published

Doctrine, skills, contracts, and the reference implementation are produced to be publishable. Inspectability is a design constraint, not a release task; the principal's private life is never part of the publication.

Implementation snapshot. The estate currently uses the local router backend with enabled: true and mode: shadow . Its recommendations are logged but do not yet override normal orchestrator judgment. The reference implementation is this estate itself; a sanitized staging repository carries the MIT licence and the publishable system.

Executive summary

FOUNDATION

OPEN SOURCE FROM DAY ONE

Open source is the foundation, not the release strategy THE VISION

Open source from day one. J.A.R.V.I.S. is built in the open as a matter of foundation, not as a release strategy. Every aim of the programme — doctrine, skills, contracts, reference implementation — is produced to be publishable, because a system that demands to be trusted must be inspectable. The boundary is absolute: the system goes public; the principal's private life never does. These papers describe a running system, not a proposal. This is an engineering constraint before it is a publishing decision. A contract that cannot survive public reading is not finished: it depends on obscurity, or on state that was never written down. Building in the open removes those hiding places at design time. That is why the vision sits here, ahead of the runtime.

What open source means, mechanically THE LICENCE

THE REPOSITORY

The MIT licence is the permission slip: anyone may use, modify, and redistribute the system, provided the copyright notice is kept. It already sits in the staging repository.

A versioned public repository is the workshop: every change is a commit with history, so any reader can see what changed, when, and why.

THE README

ISSUES AND PULL REQUESTS

The front door: what the machine is, what problem it solves, and how to run it. The staging repository already carries one.

The suggestion box and the workbench: outsiders file defects, engineers propose improvements, and the operator accepts or declines each on evidence.

Publishable by construction RULE

ENGINEERING CONSEQUENCE

Write every artifact as if it will be read in public

Skills, contracts, and doctrine carry no private names, addresses, credentials, or family detail; sanitization is a review step, not a rewrite.

The system publishes; the estate does not

Code, skills, contracts, and these papers are publishable. Memory, finances, credentials, and personal data never enter the staging tree.

The reference implementation is the estate

There is no separate demonstration system. The running estate is the implementation the papers describe — so the published system is the tested system.

Publication timing stays with the operator

Open from day one governs how the system is built. Public release remains an exact, approval-gated consequence, taken on the operator's word.

Why this strengthens the backend. Inspectability forces the rest of this paper into existence: filesystem contracts, versionable skills, evidence packets, and honest limitations are what a public reader demands. A closed system can assert reliability; an open one has to demonstrate it.

Foundation: open source

ARCHITECTURE

RUNTIME AND TRUST BOUNDARIES

A persistent host supports a deliberately narrow control plane The estate runs on one persistent Linux virtual machine. The home directory is durable across restarts; state outside it is ephemeral. The orchestrator has access to a shell, filesystem, a real Chromium browser, and subagents that it may brief and verify.

Runtime and trust boundaries

CONTROL FLOW

State machine STATE

ENTRY CONDITION

ALLOWED EXIT

Received

A direct brief or authorized scheduled trigger exists.

Routed, deferred for one focused question, or rejected as out of scope.

In progress

A bounded plan and tools are selected.

Verification, blocked, or awaiting approval.

Awaiting approval

The next operation is consequential.

Execute exactly the approved action, or stop.

Verified

Acceptance evidence has been inspected.

Handed off with evidence packet.

Failed

Required source, permission, validation, or retry budget is unavailable.

Escalate with the observed failure and the next safe option.

No optimistic completion. Queued, running, active, or delegated are intermediate states. Completion exists only after observed output passes the stated acceptance test.

Request lifecycle

PERSISTENCE

FILESYSTEM CONTRACTS

The directory tree is part of the API Durable paths define ownership, visibility, and lifecycle. A path is not clerical metadata; it is the contract that determines whether a future run can locate, revise, or safely discard state. ~ ├── MEMORY.md # curated durable facts and commitments ├── memory/ │ ├── people/ · groups/ # relationship indexes and pages │ ├── bank/ · index/ # runtime-owned, read-only │ └── <dated notes> └── workspace/ ├── skills/<name>/SKILL.md # reusable capability playbooks ├── goals/<goal-slug>/ │ ├── GOAL.md # operator notes │ ├── files/ # user-facing deliverables │ └── hidden_files/ # logs, watermarks, run state ├── usage-router/ │ ├── config.json │ └── logs/router-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl └── your_files/<slug>/ # ordinary file artifacts

STORE

DURABILITY

AUDIENCE

~/workspace/skills

Persistent

Runtime / engineers

Capture only a proven, reusable method.

Goal files/

Persistent

HgX

Create deliverables in their final home; do not maintain drifting copies.

Goal hidden_files/

Persistent

Runtime

Keep operational state out of the user-facing document surface.

MEMORY.md

Persistent

Orchestrator

Curate durable facts; reconcile corrections.

/tmp and system paths

Ephemeral

Process-local

Use only for disposable intermediates.

~/workspace/jarvisestate

Persistent

Public, staged

Sanitized publishable system only: licence, README, doctrine, and skills. Private state never enters this tree.

Artifact surfaces Ordinary file artifacts are rooted at ~/workspace/your_files/<slug>/ . Goal documents are rooted inside the goal's files/ subtree. Hosted web artifacts execute on a separate hosted runtime and may expose an optional public link. The open-source staging tree at ~/workspace/jarvis-estate/ is a separate surface with the opposite default: nothing enters it unless it is fit to be public.

Engineering consequence. Moving a final artifact after publication can break its reference; copying it creates two mutable truths. The final home is selected before the first user-facing byte is written.

On-disk contract Each skill lives at ~/workspace/skills/<name>/SKILL.md . Frontmatter contains name and description ; the description is the trigger used to decide when the skill loads. The body defines Purpose, Workflow, Output Contract, and Operating Rules. Because a skill is a plain-text contract with no hidden state, it is also the unit of open-source publication: the sanitized registry is what an outside engineer receives, reviews, and improves. --name: "capability-name" description: "Trigger: the tasks and conditions this skill owns." --# Capability Name ## Purpose ## Workflow ## Output Contract ## Operating Rules

Skill lifecycle and registry

AUTOMATION

SCHEDULING AND ROUTINES

Only reviewed work earns a schedule Cron owns recurring work. A routine is not merely a timer; it is a schedule bound to a tested skill, a live source, a failure policy, and an approval boundary. Owner

The bot or service accountable for the run and its report.

Schedule

The cadence plus an explicit timezone. Current examples include 30-minute inbox scans, an 08:15 America/Toronto morning briefing, sports briefings, and an hourly campaign run.

Live input

The current source that must be re-read at execution time.

Expected result

The artifact, decision packet, alert, or valid no-output outcome.

Failure rule

When a required source is stale or unavailable, report failure. Do not silently reuse old data.

Approval boundary

The exact final action that may not proceed unattended.

Silent-by-default is an output contract

0

1

A routine that finds no actionable change returns no user-visible output. Silence is deliberate, not

Ownership remains singular even when the work invokes tools or delegates bounded

missing observability.

subtasks.

MESSAGES ON A QUIET RUN

OWNER PER ROUTINE

Change control. A schedule edit is a contract edit. Preserve unrelated instructions, update the complete routine body, and verify the saved job before asserting that future runs changed.

Operational states SCHEDULED

RUNNING

SILENT SUCCESS

REPORTED RESULT

BLOCKED

PAUSED

A run is complete only when the execution result is observed. A queued or started job is not completion.

Scheduling and routines

STATE

MEMORY AND CONTINUITY

Memory is curated state, not an infinite transcript Three persistence classes

RECONCILIATION RULE

CLASS

CONTENTS

READ POLICY

Durable memory

Stable facts, preferences, commitments, named relationships.

Load when prior decisions or history matter.

Run logs, ledgers, watermarks, drafts, source snapshots.

Read from the owning goal or service.

Prices, schedules, account state, current records.

Re-read at decision time; do not trust memory.

Working state

Live source

Corrections replace contradictions When HgX corrects a fact, the memory system reconciles the old note rather than appending a second incompatible version. The objective is a usable current record, not an archaeology of mistakes.

Storage map ~/MEMORY.md

Curated long-term record: facts, preferences, commitments, and durable operating decisions.

~/memory/

Dated notes plus people and group indexes. Detailed relationship pages consolidate facts for the named subject.

bank/ · index/

Runtime-owned memory infrastructure. Read-only to the operating agent.

Side chats

Independent context scopes aligned to bot duties. A bot's work and memory do not cross into another bot's role.

Security limitation. Side-chat isolation is procedural. Separate roles, conversations, and memory scopes improve relevance and discretion, but do not establish cryptographic isolation on a shared machine.

Continuity rule The orchestrator consults memory before acting on prior work, but uses the original files and live systems as the authority for changing or consequential facts. Memory answers “what matters”; the source system answers “what is true now.” Memory is also the clearest case of the open-source boundary: it is the principal's private life in durable form, so it is never staged, never sanitized into publication, and never leaves the estate.

Memory and continuity

COST CONTROL

USAGE ROUTER

The router is a gate, not a second orchestrator The usage router is consulted before live-browser work, deep research, a retry of a failed approach, or spawning a subagent. It decides whether the proposed path is the cheapest reliable way to satisfy the brief; it never approves consequences.

JSONL DECISION RECORD / SHADOW ADVISES · ACTIVE ENFORCES

Policy order #

RULE

ACTION

1

Irreversible or consequential effect

Escalate to HgX; the router never authorizes it.

2

Same operation, same error, two retries consumed

Escalate or choose a fundamentally different approach.

3

Same action proposed three times without measurable progress

Deny and report the loop.

4

Fresh cached artifact fully answers the goal

Use cache; do not refetch.

5

Subagent candidate is not multi-step, independent, and longrunning

Deny delegation; execute directly.

6

Browser or deep research lacks a live-state requirement

Deny expensive path; use search or local evidence.

7

High-cost work begins from a vague goal

Defer for one focused question.

Current deployment: local backend, enabled, shadow mode.

Usage router architecture

ROUTING CONTRACT

SCHEMA AND EXECUTION

A compact decision state keeps routing inspectable { "goal": "what HgX wants", "kind": "chat|lookup|research|browser|coding|write|account", "cached_artifact": false, "cached_note": "freshness and scope, if any", "prior_error": "last error, if retrying", "same_error_count": 0, "sources_found": 0, "constraints": "limits that apply" }

Mode semantics MODE

BEHAVIOR

Shadow

Compute and log a recommendation; normal orchestrator judgment still chooses the action.

Active

Honor the router action, except that approval and security rules remain authoritative.

Bypassed

Skip consultation and follow the normal orchestrator path.

Decision log Every consultation appends one record to logs/router-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl .

{ "ts": "…", "mode": "shadow|active", "state": { "…": "…" }, "recommendation": "…", "action_taken": "…", "note": "…" }

Optional TypeSafe Jev backend Jev is a System One model for typed decisions, not a replacement language model. The optional integration may send only the compact decision state and typed questions. The API key is supplied through Secure Vault, never ordinary chat, files, logs, or generated code. Backend failure returns immediately to the local policy and records the fallback.

External-backend boundary. Enabling Jev introduces a network dependency and sends the compact task state to TypeSafe. The estate therefore keeps the local policy as both default and fallback.

Router pseudocode if disabled or explicit_bypass: return NORMAL_PATH state = compact(task, cache, prior_error, source_count, constraints) route = local_policy(state) or optional_jev(state) append_jsonl(timestamp, mode, state, route) if mode == "shadow": return orchestrator_judgment return enforce(route) # approval rules still dominate

Router data contract

ORCHESTRATION

TEAM DEPLOYMENT

The minimum team executes; the orchestrator remains accountable HgX briefs one Chief-of-Staff orchestrator. It decomposes the objective into real jobs, reuses existing specialists, creates only the roles needed now, and parallelizes only independent work. Brief contract OUTCOME

CAPABILITY

BOUNDARY

Project, objective, deadline,

Available tools, integrations,

Constraints, exclusions,

audience, and measurable success criteria.

existing specialists, and required live sources.

budget, approval points, and stop conditions.

Worker contract: one agent, one job FIELD

REQUIRED DEFINITION

Mission

One bounded responsibility that does not overlap another worker.

Inputs

The source files, facts, and constraints needed for that responsibility.

Tools

Only the capabilities necessary to produce the assigned output.

Expected output

A concrete artifact, finding, patch, or evidence packet.

Stop condition

Done, blocked, or approval needed.

Proof

Sources, tests, changed files, or other observable completion evidence.

Coordination algorithm jobs = decompose(brief) team = minimum_existing_specialists(jobs) parallel = independent(jobs) results = execute(team, parallel) for result in results: verify(result.artifact, result.sources, result.tests, result.gaps) if consequential_next_step: request_exact_approval(target, effect) return handoff(what_built, agents_and_why, links, evidence, gaps, next_steps)

Standing rule. No subagent publishes, sends, spends, or deletes without HgX's approval. A persuasive status message without proof has no standing in the completion state.

Control matrix SURFACE

CONTROL

NON-GOAL

Consequential actions

Approval card at the last safe moment; scope limited to the stated target and effect.

Approval does not authorize adjacent actions or future repetitions.

Credentials

Secure Vault capture; secrets excluded from chat, files, logs, code, and reports.

The vault is not bypassed by convenience or delegation.

One-time codes

Protected read and fill path for the confirmed current challenge.

Raw codes are not exposed or retained.

Connectors

Scoped OAuth permissions; read, write, and sharing scopes may be granted separately.

A connected service is not blanket authorization.

Side chats

Procedural role and memory isolation.

Not a cryptographic boundary on the shared host.

External router

Compact state only; key held in Secure Vault; local fallback.

Jev never performs or approves the downstream action.

Approval object { "operation": "the exact action", "target": "the named recipient, system, or object", "scope": "the values and files included", "effect": "what will change outside the estate", "status": "requested|approved|denied|expired" }

This is a conceptual contract for review; the paper does not assert a particular internal wire format.

Honest boundary. Role separation limits accidental disclosure and context drift. It does not isolate files, sessions, or credentials by process, user, container, or encryption domain. Open source adds one further control in the same spirit: sanitization is reviewed before anything enters the staging tree, and a private value found there is a security defect, not an editorial one.

Approvals and security

EVIDENCE AND OBSERVABILITY

Evidence is the completion protocol Every consequential run returns an evidence packet that another engineer can inspect without reconstructing the conversation. The packet is both the acceptance interface and the raw material for operational review.

Operator scorecard MEASURE

QUESTION ANSWERED

EVIDENCE SOURCE

Accepted work

Skill tests and routine history.

Decision telemetry The usage router writes append-only JSON Lines records by date. Routine runs retain their own execution history. Goal-specific logs and watermarks remain in hidden_files/ , separate from user-facing deliverables. These stores answer different questions and should not be conflated into one undifferentiated transcript.

Observability principle. Silence may be the correct user-facing result, but it must not erase internal evidence that the routine ran, found no actionable change, or failed.

Evidence and observability

RESILIENCE

FAILURE SEMANTICS

Explicit failure is safer than plausible continuity FAILURE CLASS

DETECTION

REQUIRED RESPONSE

Stale or unavailable live source

The owning system cannot return current data for the run.

Report the failure; do not substitute remembered or cached state unless the contract explicitly permits it.

Repeated identical error

The same operation returns the same error twice.

Stop same-path retries; escalate or select a fundamentally different approach.

Loop without progress

The same action is proposed three times with no measurable change.

Deny the loop and surface what was attempted.

Delegation without proof

A worker reports completion without inspectable evidence.

Keep the task open; verify or rerun.

Missing approval

The next action sends, publishes, spends, deletes, changes permissions, or creates another consequence.

Pause at the last safe moment and request exact approval.

Credential challenge

A password, key, passkey, or one-time code is required.

Use the protected credential flow; never request or persist the raw value in ordinary state.

Router backend unavailable

The optional external decision service fails or is disabled.

Fall back to local policy and log the fallback.

Idempotency and side effects The system's strongest retry rule is to avoid repeating an unobserved consequential action. Approval and evidence must identify the target and effect before execution; a retry follows only when the prior result establishes that the action did not complete, or when the underlying operation is known to be safely repeatable.

FAIL CLOSED

FAIL VISIBLE

Credentials, permissions, deletion, publication, sending, and spending do not proceed on

Source errors and validation failures are reported with the observed condition, not

ambiguous authorization or ambiguous prior

concealed behind a stale artifact or fabricated

outcome.

completion.

Minimum incident record { time, owner, objective, last_safe_state, attempted_operation, observed_error, same_error_count, artifacts_preserved, approval_state, next_safe_option }

Conceptual fields only. The deployed estate uses existing run logs and evidence packets rather than a separate incident database.

Failure semantics

SENIOR ENGINEERING REVIEW

ACCEPTANCE CHECKLIST

Review the contracts before reviewing the prose Control plane One visible owner exists for the final result. Expensive paths pass through the routing decision. Independent jobs are parallelized; dependent jobs are sequenced. Delegated results are verified before handoff.

Persistence Durable state is rooted under the home directory. User-facing and internal goal files remain separated. Ephemeral paths contain no required future state. Corrections reconcile previous memory.

Retry budget

Same operation produces the same error three times.

Third repetition is not executed; action escalates or changes fundamentally.

Stale source

Approval scope

Drafted external action is ready to send.

Execution pauses with target, scope, and effect visible.

Credential hygiene

Workflow reaches a login or API-key requirement.

Secure capture is requested; raw secret is absent from chat and logs.

Delegation integrity

Subagent returns a completion claim without evidence.

Orchestrator rejects completion and requests proof or reruns validation.

Silent routine

No actionable change exists.

No user alert; internal run evidence remains available.

A skill or contract is staged for open-source publication.

Acceptance tests

CONSTRAINTS

LIMITATIONS AND RISK POSTURE

The design is intentionally useful before it is institution-grade Single operator

The estate is designed around HgX as the principal and final authority. It does not define multi-party authorization, separation of duties, or organization-wide policy administration.

Procedural isolation

Bot roles and side-chat memories are separated by operating discipline. They are not process, account, container, or cryptographic isolation boundaries.

Approval latency

Consequential work intentionally waits for HgX. Safety improves, but end-to-end completion time depends on operator availability.

Alert fatigue

As scheduled services multiply, even correct reports can consume the operator's attention. Silent-by-default behavior and narrow exception thresholds are therefore core controls.

Open-source staging

The system is built to be publishable, but publication itself is staged: a sanitized repository is prepared and reviewed, and the public release proceeds only on the operator's word. Nothing in this paper asserts a completed public release.

What this design does not claim It does not claim cryptographic tenancy boundaries between roles on the shared runtime. It does not claim that a model's recommendation is deterministic, correct, or self-authenticating. It does not claim universal savings from an external routing backend. It does not replace provider security documentation, account controls, or source-system authorization. It does not make unattended consequential actions acceptable merely because a workflow is repeatable.

Risk-reduction direction The next engineering gains should come from measuring the existing scorecard, reviewing shadow-router decisions before active enforcement, pruning noisy routines, and testing role boundaries under realistic failure cases. More agents are not a reliability strategy. More accepted work, lower repair cost, and cleaner evidence are.

Architectural posture. The estate favors reversible preparation, explicit state, bounded retries, and visible human control over nominal autonomy. This costs a little ceremony. Ceremony is cheaper than unexplained side effects.

Limitations

Paper III — Front-End White Paper: The Operator Interface

VERSION 1.1 · FULL TEXT

E S TAT E E N G I N E E R I N G S E R I E S / PA P E R I I I

J.A.R.V.I.S. Front-End White Paper The Operator Interface Across the Apple Ecosystem VERSION

AUDIENCE

1.1 / October 2026

Senior front-end engineers, platform architects, accessibility engineers, and design-system owners.

The scarcest resource is not compute. It is the operator's attention. This paper defines the interface layer that protects that attention while making evidence, authority, and system state legible. Main vision — open source from day one. The interface described here is not a closed product surface. It is the public face of an open system: built in the open, inspectable by anyone, and publishable by design. The system goes public; the principal’s private life never does.

J.A.R.V.I.S. / FRONT-END ENGINEERING

READER MAP

The trilogy closes the operator gap The three papers describe one system from three different control planes. None is complete without the other two. I / Operating architecture J.A.R.V.I.S. — An Operating System for the HgX Estate, v1.2 Defines vision — with open source from day one as its foundation — role federation, the seven-stage doctrine, human authority, evidence, and team deployment.

II / Backend engineering

III / Operator interface

J.A.R.V.I.S. — Technical White Paper: Backend Engineering, v1.1

This paper

Defines runtime boundaries, filesystem and skill contracts, scheduling, memory, routing, approvals, and evidence.

Defines how those contracts become calm, device-native, openly inspectable interaction across iPhone, Watch, iPad, TV, and Vision Pro. This paper, v1.1.

Reading contract The operating paper answers what the estate is and why it exists. The backend paper answers how accepted work is executed and proven. This paper answers how HgX perceives, controls, and reviews that work. It neither replaces governance with interface polish nor allows a client to become a second orchestrator. All three papers now share one main vision: open source from day one. The full foundation statement is stated in the executive summary that follows, and every section of this paper is written to be publishable against it. Front-end thesis. The interface is an attention-protection system first and a control surface second. It should reveal state without manufacturing urgency, expose evidence without requiring archaeology, and stop cleanly at consequences.

TRILOGY / SCOPE

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

The front end governs attention, not intelligence J.A.R.V.I.S. already has doctrine and a control plane. The interface must make them perceivable without leaking their complexity. One estate, several views iPhone commands. Watch gates. iPad reviews. TV shows state. Vision Pro spatializes. Each surface renders the same contracts with a different disclosure and interaction budget.

3 questions Every screen must answer: 1. What changed? 2. What needs me?

Contracts before clients

3. What proves it?

Clients consume versioned status, evidence, approval, decision, and artifact contracts. They do not call models, inspect private files, or infer state from conversational text. Model and vendor changes stay behind the backend boundary.

Quiet is a valid output Silent-by-default becomes a first-class UI state. A quiet day produces no alert and no theatrical activity; absence of signal is not a defect.

1 owner The orchestrator owns the handoff. A device may present work; it does not redefine it, bypass approval, or become a source of truth.

Foundation — open source is the main vision Open source from day one. J.A.R.V.I.S. is built in the open as a matter of foundation, not as a release strategy. Every aim of the programme — doctrine, skills, contracts, reference implementation — is produced to be publishable, because a system that demands to be trusted must be inspectable. The boundary is absolute: the system goes public; the principal's private life never does. These papers describe a running system, not a proposal.

Concretely: the contracts, tokens, components, and reference clients here are produced to be publishable, so anyone can inspect — and reproduce — how state, evidence, and approval are rendered. The reference implementation is the live system running the HgX estate itself.

Engineering invariants Invariant

Front-end consequence

No proof, no done

Completion requires evidence metadata, not a success message.

Current facts stay live

Views display freshness; stale data cannot masquerade as current.

Approval binds to consequence

Cards show target and effect; stale or mutated intents cannot be approved.

Roles remain narrow

Navigation follows estate roles; cross-role status is read-only.

Failures remain visible

Degraded and unknown are distinct, recoverable states.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

F R O N T- E N D P H I L O S O P H Y

Calm technology earns the right to interrupt The interface must reduce supervisory effort rather than turn autonomous work into a new inbox. Because the system is open, that calm is verifiable: the rules that decide when to interrupt are published, not asserted. Silent by default

Glanceable over browsable

No change and no decision means no uservisible event. A routine that completed quietly should not demand acknowledgement merely to prove it ran.

State appears before detail. A reader can stop at the summary, expand evidence, or inspect raw provenance only when warranted.

Progress without theatre

Language is part of control

Queued, delegated, and running are honest intermediate states. Animated thinking, fake percentages, and fabricated completion times are excluded.

Microcopy is formal, precise, and dry. It distinguishes fact from estimate and a paused service from a failed one. Wit never enters an approval boundary.

Attention budget State

Default rendering

Operator burden

No pixels; optionally a quiet aggregate in Watchtower

Review when convenient

Approval card on a personal surface

Degraded source

State plus observed failure and safe next step

Intervene only if outcome is threatened

Unknown

Neutral uncertainty; never infer outage

Inspect or wait

Microcopy examples. “No action required.” “Source unavailable; no cached value substituted.” “Prepared. Awaiting approval to send.” “Paused by HgX; this is not an outage.” The voice is part of the operating contract. The operating paper calls the destination calm competence. The backend paper gives that phrase mechanics— live sources, explicit states, retry budgets, and evidence. The front end completes the chain by refusing to turn mechanics into noise.

PHILOSOPHY / ATTENTION

O P E R AT O R L O O P

The doctrine becomes five interaction states The operating paper's chain—ROLE → LIVE SOURCE → TOOL → ARTIFACT → EVIDENCE → APPROVAL → ROUTINE—is not exposed as a wizard. It compiles into a shorter operator loop while the backend preserves the full chain of custody.

BRIEF

one clean request

WORK

›

inspectable on demand

EVIDENCE

›

artifact, sources, gaps

APPROVAL

›

target and effect

DECISION

›

result and proof

1 / Brief

4 / Approval

The command surface captures objective, constraints, audience, and finish line. It asks one focused question only when the missing fact changes execution.

Every irreversible or external action pauses at the last safe moment. The card is bound to an immutable intent digest; any target or effect change invalidates approval.

2 / Work The default is a compact state: accepted, in progress, blocked, or awaiting approval. Drill-down exposes role, current source, route, and last meaningful event. It never streams private reasoning.

3 / Evidence The review layer leads with the artifact, then provenance, changes, tests, approvals, and gaps— the evidence packet defined by the backend paper.

5 / Decision packet The final handoff states what happened, what proves it, what remains, and what should happen next. Completion is a state transition supported by evidence, not a sentence generated by a model.

Recovery Network interruption never converts “pending” to “approved.” Clients resynchronize against server state using idempotency keys and revision tokens.

State transition rule received → accepted → in_progress → {awaiting_approval | blocked | verifying} awaiting_approval + valid_approval → executing → verifying verifying + acceptance_passed → completed any state + evidence_of_failure → failed paused is operator intent; unknown is insufficient observation; neither equals failed

INTERACTION / STATE MACHINE

AT O M I C S A F E T Y C O N T R O L

An approval card is not a prettier confirm dialog It is the user interface representation of the consequence boundary defined by both companion papers. Required fields

A P P R O VA L R E Q U I R E D

Publish the prepared document Target Public distribution endpoint

Action: a verb with an object. Target: destination, recipient, account, or record. Effect: what becomes visible, binding, spent, deleted, or changed. Evidence: relevant artifact and checks.

Effect Makes version 1.0 externally accessible to anyone with its link. Why now Artifact passed document validation and is ready for release. Evidence 1 artifact · 3 checks · 0 known gaps Intent expires after 10 minutes. Any content or destination change requires a new approval.

Scope: one action, quantity, and expiry. Reversibility: recovery path where one exists.

Prohibited patterns Bare “Are you sure?” dialogs. Preselected consent or approval bundled with unrelated choices. Approving a class of future actions from a single intent. Colour-only risk signaling. Approval while target or effect is truncated.

Deny

Approve once

Security semantics Event

Required behavior

Intent changes

Invalidate the card; issue a new intent identifier and digest.

Card expires

Disable approval; refresh current source and effect.

Offline tap

Record no local approval. Re-fetch and present current intent.

Duplicate tap

Idempotency prevents duplicate execution; render the authoritative result.

Device cannot show full effect

Deep-link to a capable personal surface; never compress away consequential detail.

CLIENT ARCHITECTURE

The client renders contracts; it never becomes the control plane APPLE CLIENTS

Authentication and device security posture.

Observation-driven state store with explicit loading, stale, empty, failed, and offline states.

TV focus engine and redaction.

Scene lifecycle and deep links.

VisionOS windows, volumes, and immersive spaces.

Telemetry that records UI and contract events without task contents.

Boundary. A SwiftUI view may request ApprovalIntent(id). It may not construct an approval from conversational text, call a model to reinterpret the effect, or walk the estate filesystem described in the backend paper. The client core, contracts, and design system are open components: anyone may read, build, and audit them, because trust in the interface must not depend on trusting its author.

Recommended module topology JarvisContracts JarvisTransport JarvisState JarvisDesignSystem JarvisEvidenceUI JarvisApprovalUI JarvisPlatform* JarvisTestKit

// versioned domain models; no UI dependencies // auth, sync, retries, event stream, clock skew // reducers/stores; explicit state transitions // tokens and semantic components // packet viewers and artifact routing // consequence-safe presentation and action // iOS, watchOS, iPadOS, tvOS, visionOS adapters // contract fixtures, accessibility, redaction, chaos

SwiftUI provides a common declarative layer across Apple platforms, but portability is treated as shared semantics rather than identical layouts.[3]

ARCHITECTURE / THIN CLIENTS

D E V I C E S T R AT E G Y / I P H O N E

iPhone is the command surface The phone accepts a brief, exposes meaningful state, presents approval, and returns the decision packet. It is not a miniature workstation. Primary jobs

Default information architecture

Capture one clean request by text or voice.

Brief: composer plus active role.

Route into the correct estate role.

Now: decisions and blocks only.

Receive only material status changes.

Estate: Watchtower summary.

Approve or deny a fully rendered intent.

Artifacts: recent accepted work.

Open the final artifact and evidence summary.

Settings: privacy, notifications, devices.

Interaction constraints Concern

Implementation

Fast command entry

Single composer with attachments and explicit destination; no mandatory form before ordinary work.

Long-running work

Return immediately with work identifier and state; resume via push or foreground sync.

Approval

Full card with target and effect visible before the action control. Require local authentication for configured risk classes.

Evidence

Progressive disclosure: summary, checks, sources, artifact. Preserve selectable text and shareable artifacts.

Offline

Read cached non-sensitive summaries. Disable consequence actions until authoritative state is restored.

Operator mode The operating paper's human-led, agent-executed model means the phone should ask for outcomes, not supervise steps. “Prepare the filing” is the normal command; a checklist editor is an escape hatch for inspection, not the default experience. Success measure. The median accepted request should require one brief, zero supervisory taps, and at most one approval. More interaction is justified only by ambiguity or consequence.

A split-view shell pairs the artifact with its evidence packet. The primary column selects role, task, or artifact; the content column renders the work; an inspector presents provenance, changes, tests, approvals, gaps, and freshness.

The technical paper's filesystem contract remains backend-only. The iPad receives artifact manifests and authorized bytes, not direct path semantics.

SURFACES / IPAD

D E V I C E S T R AT E G Y / A P P L E T V

Apple TV is the ambient wall, not a surveillance console The Watchtower becomes a calm shared-room display: state at a distance, contents nowhere in sight. E S TAT E S TAT E

Disclosure ceiling

Nominal

No message bodies.

Observed services are operational or intentionally paused.

No recipient or account names.

No financial, health, legal, or betting values. No artifact previews.

Runtime

No approval controls.

Heartbeat observed

No historical activity trail beyond coarse trend.

Routines

Distance rules

Paused by operator

Typography and focus states must remain legible across a room. Navigation is shallow: overview, role states, runtime proof, and freshness. Detailed inspection deep-links to a personal device.

External page State not verified

Watchtower mapping The implementation's four states—operational, paused, degraded, unknown—must remain semantically distinct. A 403 that prevents verification is unknown, not degraded. A routine deliberately stopped by HgX is paused, not down. The front end must preserve these distinctions or it will manufacture incidents. Privacy rule. Shared screens show states, never contents. Even “harmless” numbers can reveal private activity through context. The television earns ambient presence by knowing less.

Operational mode Use a low-refresh dashboard, burn-in-aware layout rotation, no autoplay sound, and a visible freshness timestamp. If data expires, the display transitions to unknown rather than retaining a reassuring stale green.

SURFACES / APPLE TV

D E V I C E S T R AT E G Y / A P P L E V I S I O N P R O

Vision Pro is the spatial estate—when it earns the role Spatial computing can externalize relationships among roles, artifacts, and evidence. It must not turn ordinary administration into theatre. Spatial model Role spaces: persistent windows for owned domains, not animated personalities. Evidence field: artifact at the centre; sources, checks, and gaps arranged by provenance. Approval object: a bounded panel that remains attached to its target and effect. Watchtower volume: a compact estate topology whose colour and shape encode state.

Progressive immersion Default to ordinary windows. Use volumes where depth carries information. Reserve immersive spaces for complex dependency inspection or incident response, never routine approval.

Input Eye, hand, pointer, and voice are presentation modalities, not authority. Gaze may focus an approval card; deliberate action and authentication still grant consent.

Safety Critical controls remain stable, comfortably placed, and reachable without large motion. The scene must tolerate interruption, recentering, and loss of tracking without mutating task state.

Privacy Vision Pro is personal by default, but guest and mirroring states require explicit redaction. Sensitive views blank or downgrade when the session is not private.

Build now, deploy later. Define spatially neutral contracts today. Add a visionOS renderer only when real tasks show that spatial arrangement reduces review time or error. Hardware novelty is not an acceptance test.

Framework posture SwiftUI should carry standard windows and controls; RealityKit should enter only where entities, depth, or spatial manipulation are the information architecture. Apple's own platform material demonstrates composition between SwiftUI views and RealityKit entities, supporting a layered rather than all-spatial implementation.[5]

SURFACES / VISION PRO

DESIGN SYSTEM

One visual language, five native dialects Consistency lives in semantics, typography, state, and consequence—not identical screen geometry. Palette

Typography

Motion

Dark-mode-first navy and graphite surfaces; cool blue for information; green operational; amber paused; red degraded; grey unknown. State always includes text or shape.

System typography with Dynamic Type. Large, restrained hierarchy; monospaced numerals only where comparison benefits. No compressed copy to rescue an overloaded screen.

Motion communicates state transition, spatial continuity, or consequence. No decorative activity loops. Reduce Motion preserves meaning through opacity, shape, and text.

Apple's cross-platform SwiftUI model supports shared declarative components and accessibility behaviors, while native platform conventions still govern focus, input, layout, and disclosure.[3] The tokens and semantic components above are published as part of the open system, so a community client can match the estate’s state language exactly — same colours, same labels, same consequence separation — without reverseengineering screenshots.

SYSTEM / VISUAL LANGUAGE

CONTRACTS / I

Status is observed truth with explicit uncertainty The Watchtower contract is the first shared read model. Its current implementation emits a generated timestamp, state counts, and service records. Front-end production should preserve that core while adding versioning and stable identifiers. { "schema_version": "1.0", "generated_at": "RFC3339 UTC", "summary": { "operational": 0, "paused": 0, "degraded": 0, "unknown": 0 }, "services": [{ "id": "stable-service-id", "display_name": "human label", "state": "operational|paused|degraded|unknown", "note": "short observed reason", "cadence": "human-readable schedule", "checked_at": "RFC3339 UTC", "fresh_until": "RFC3339 UTC?", "evidence_ref": "opaque reference?" }] }

Rendering rules Field

Rule

generated_at / checked_at

Render freshness; when expired, lower confidence rather than retaining the prior state indefinitely.

state

Exhaustive enum. Unknown values render as unknown and emit compatibility telemetry.

note

Human-readable explanation; clients never parse it to derive state.

summary

Convenience only. Clients may recompute counts and flag mismatches.

evidence_ref

Opaque identifier resolved through authorized evidence transport, never a local path.

Compatibility. Additive fields are tolerated. Enum expansion fails closed to unknown. Removing or reinterpreting a field requires a new major schema. Clients advertise supported versions during sync. This contract directly renders the backend paper's failure semantics: paused is intentional, degraded is observed impairment, and unknown is insufficient evidence. The operating paper's “truth over comfort” principle is therefore enforced at the type boundary. It is also a public specification: the status contract is part of the open-source release, versioned in the open, so external clients and auditors work from the same truth as the estate’s own surfaces.

CONTRACTS / WATCHTOWER

CONTRACTS / II

Evidence and decision packets make completion reviewable Evidence packet

Decision packet

{

{

"id": "ev_…", "work_id": "wk_…", "revision": 7, "artifact_refs": ["ar_…"], "sources": [{"label":"…","uri":"…", "retrieved_at":"…"}], "changes": [{"kind":"created|updated", "summary":"…"}], "tests": [{"name":"…","state":"passed|failed", "evidence":"…"}], "approvals": ["ap_…"], "gaps": [{"severity":"…","summary":"…"}], "created_at": "…" }

"id": "dp_…", "work_id": "wk_…", "outcome": "completed|blocked|failed|cancelled", "summary": "observed result", "evidence_ref": "ev_…", "approval_refs": ["ap_…"], "remaining": [{"summary":"…","owner":"…"}], "next_safe_action": "…", "completed_at": "…", "trace_id": "opaque correlation id" }

Client rules Evidence is immutable per revision. A corrected artifact produces a new packet revision. A decision packet cannot claim completed if required tests failed or a critical gap remains. Sources display as human labels and authorized links; internal tool identifiers never become citations. Missing evidence renders as missing evidence. The client does not fabricate a success explanation. Trace identifiers support diagnostics but never appear on shared surfaces. Relationship to the trilogy. The operating paper defines evidence as the unit of trust. The backend paper defines the evidence envelope and acceptance state. This paper defines progressive disclosure, platform presentation, and the interaction that returns the reviewer to the exact claim.

Rendering strategy iPhone shows summary and gaps first. Watch shows only whether an attached approval has sufficient evidence. iPad exposes the complete packet. TV shows state only. Vision Pro may spatialize provenance, but the underlying ordering and identifiers remain unchanged.

CONTRACTS / EVIDENCE

CONTRACTS / III

Approval intents and artifact manifests isolate consequence from presentation Approval intent

Artifact manifest

{

{

"id": "ap_…", "work_id": "wk_…", "action": "publish", "target": {"type":"destination", "display":"public link"}, "effect": "Makes artifact externally accessible", "risk_class": "external_write", "artifact_revision": 7, "evidence_ref": "ev_…", "expires_at": "…", "intent_digest": "sha256:…", "allowed_decisions": ["approve","deny"] }

"id": "ar_…", "work_id": "wk_…", "revision": 7, "name": "Front-End White Paper", "media_type": "application/pdf", "byte_length": 0, "content_digest": "sha256:…", "classification": "personal|shared|public", "created_at": "…", "download": {"url":"short-lived authorized URL", "expires_at":"…"} }

Transport and synchronization Concern

Contract

Authentication

Device-bound session plus platform authentication appropriate to risk; credentials never enter task payloads.

Authorization

Server evaluates principal, device, surface class, action, and object revision.

Mutation

Idempotency key, expected revision, intent digest, and explicit result state.

Events

Push carries an opaque event identifier and minimal preview; clients fetch current authorized state.

Offline

Cache read-only summaries by classification; queue no consequential mutation.

Deletion

Local cache purges on sign-out, classification change, revocation, or expiry.

The backend paper owns execution and evidence. The client owns faithful display, local security posture, and an unambiguous operator gesture. Approval is not a boolean sent by a view; it is a signed response to a specific, current intent.

CONTRACTS / APPROVAL AND ARTIFACTS

N O T I F I C AT I O N D I S C I P L I N E

Interrupt only when delay changes the outcome The usage router's escalation path feeds a three-tier notification policy. The client does not promote urgency merely because a task finished. Tier

Criterion

Surface

Examples

Wrist buzz

A decision is needed now; delay risks the result or a stated deadline.

Watch + iPhone alert

Time-bound approval; material security anomaly; deadline inside the approved threshold.

Badge

Worth knowing today; no immediate consequence.

iPhone/iPad badge

Completed artifact; non-urgent blocked task; degraded routine with safe fallback.

Silent

Context for the next briefing or no actionable change.

Briefing/Watchtower only

Routine success; no-change watch; informational result; paused service.

Routine mapping Routine class

Default tier

Promotion rule

Inbox triage

Silent

Wrist only for direct human reply need, imminent deadline, or anomalous security signal.

Morning briefing

Badge / scheduled delivery

Watchtower sweep

Silent

Badge for degraded; wrist only when a critical outcome is threatened.

Delivery mechanics Push payloads contain minimal preview data and an opaque event identifier. Notification categories map to safe actions: open, defer, acknowledge. Approval appears inline only when the entire target and effect fit the surface contract. Deduplicate by work identifier and state revision. Collapse superseded updates. Respect Focus and platform notification settings; never create an in-app shadow notification system to defeat them. Apple platforms support lock-screen visibility and quick actions, which makes payload minimization and preview redaction part of the data design, not merely notification styling.[6]

ATTENTION / NOTIFICATIONS

C O R R E C T N E S S / A C C E S S I B I L I T Y A N D P R I VA C Y

Safe approval requires perceivable meaning and bounded disclosure Accessibility is correctness VoiceOver-first cards: announce action, target, effect, risk, expiry, then controls. “Approve” includes the action in its accessibility label.

Surface disclosure matrix Surface

Allowed disclosure

iPhone

Dynamic Type: target and effect reflow without truncation; buttons remain reachable at accessibility sizes.

Full authorized content; lock-screen previews remain minimal.

Watch

Reduce Motion: state changes use text, shape, and opacity rather than movement.

Target + effect only; no message bodies or sensitive values.

iPad

Full authorized content; review-desk controls.

High contrast: pass contrast requirements in every state and material.

TV

Differentiate without colour: operational, paused, degraded, and unknown have unique glyphs and labels.

States only; never private contents or approval.

Vision Pro

Full personal content; redact in guest, capture, or mirroring conditions.

Focus integrity: updates do not move assistive focus before a decision.

Threat model at the presentation layer Threat

Mitigation

Shoulder surfing / shared room

Surface classification, scene-aware redaction, minimal notifications, rapid privacy cover.

Stale approval

Expiry, revision check, digest binding, authoritative refresh.

Confusable target

Canonical display name, type, destination, and secondary discriminator.

Screen capture

Classify sensitive views, suppress where platform permits, audit distribution through artifact manifests.

Accessibility omission

Automated labels and contrast checks plus manual VoiceOver task completion.

Apple provides VoiceOver, text scaling, contrast, reduced-motion, and non-colour differentiation across its accessibility ecosystem.[7] WidgetKit also supports privacy redaction for highly visible widgets and complications.[4] The backend paper's least-privilege rule therefore extends all the way to pixels.

CORRECTNESS / INCLUSIVE PRIVACY

ENGINEERING DELIVERY

Contract conformance gates every surface Test pyramid Schema: decode current and previous majorcompatible fixtures; unknown enums fail to neutral states. Reducer: property tests prohibit illegal transitions and optimistic completion. Snapshot: Dynamic Type, localisation expansion, dark/light, high contrast, and redaction. Accessibility: VoiceOver order, labels, traits, actions, focus retention, and five-second Watch comprehension. Contract: client against a deterministic edge simulator, including clock skew and expiry. Chaos: duplicated events, out-of-order revisions, expired URLs, network loss during approval, and stale status.

Release gates No client can approve a mutated or expired intent. No shared surface reveals restricted fields. No unknown state renders as operational. Every completed work item opens a valid evidence packet. Every artifact digest matches downloaded bytes. Every consequential action is idempotent. Every critical flow completes with VoiceOver and large text. Nothing ships that cannot be published: no private data, credential, or unpublished dependency enters a client, fixture, or screenshot.

Roadmap: scale contracts before hardware Phase

Deliverable

Exit criterion

Now

iPhone command surface; status, work state, evidence summary, decision packet — released as the open reference client.

Real requests complete with lower interaction and no weaker approval control.

Next A

Watch triage, complications, and bounded approvals.

Five-second comprehension; zero sensitivecontent leakage in lock/always-on states.

Next B

iPad split-view evidence review.

Independent reviewer can verify an artifact without reconstructing chat.

Later A

Apple TV Watchtower dashboard.

Useful at room distance; states only; stale state visibly degrades.

Later B

Vision Pro spatial evidence workspace.

Measured reduction in review time or error over iPad.

Team deployment. The operating paper's Chief-of-Staff pattern applies: contract engineer, platform engineer, accessibility engineer, security reviewer, and test engineer receive one job each. The backend paper's evidence envelope governs the return. No proof, no release.

DELIVERY / ROADMAP

REFERENCES AND CLOSING POSITION

The front end is the thinnest part of a correct system When the contracts are right, a new device is a rendering choice—not a new operating system, a new source of truth, or a new authority boundary. The operating paper gives the estate its purpose. The backend paper gives it reliable mechanics. This paper gives HgX a calm place to stand — in the open, where anyone can inspect how that calm is earned. Document status: this paper describes the live, running J.A.R.V.I.S. system — the estate itself is the reference implementation, not a hypothetical. The iPhone interaction model reflects current operator practice; dedicated native clients for Watch, iPad, TV, and Vision Pro remain roadmap components until implemented and verified. Publication of the system proceeds under the open-source foundation stated in this paper; the principal’s private life is never part of that publication.
